 الباب األول 
 مقدمة 
 كليه الهندسة بجامعة الريادة للعلوم والتكنولوجيا
هي 
 إحدى كليات الجامعة
والتي 
 تقع بالمحور
المركزي 
 بمدينة
 السادات . 
( مادة1
): رﺅﻳة الكلية 
( تسعى كلية الهندسة بجامعة الريادة للعلوم والتكنولوجياRST
  ) أن تكون مركزًا رائدًا
في  
 التعليم الهندسي 
 والبحث العلمي، وذات أثر إيجابي مستدام على التنمية واالقتصاد 
 .الوطني 
( مادة2
): رسالة الكلية 
 ( تلتزم كلية الهندسة في جامعة الريادة للعلوم والتكنولوجياRST
 ) بتقديم خدمات تعليمية وبحثية متميزة، وتخريج
،كوادر هندسية محترفة وباحثين مؤهلين 
 ملتزمين باألخالقيات المهنية والقيم،المجتمعية 
 يساهمون في تطوير
.المجتمع والبيئة المحيطة من خالل تقديم حلول مبتكرة لمشكالت المجتمع والصناعة 
( مادة3
): ال قيم المحورﻳة 
 تلتزم جامعة الريادة للعلوم والتكنولوجيا وكلياتها المختلفة بمجموعة من القيم المحورية
التي 
 تحكم منظومة
  األداء وبيئة العمل داخل الجامعة وتستند اليها جميع السياسات واإلجراءات  
 واألنشطة األكاديمية واإلدارية
 والمالية بالجامعة، وأهم القيم ما
يلي:  
•
 
 األمانة والنزاهة 
 
                              
Honesty and Integrity
 
•
 
 الشفافية والمساءلة 
             
Transparency and Accountability
 
•
 
 التعاون والتواصل 
 
Collaboration and Communication
 
•
 
 التطوير المستمر 
 
                         
Continuous Improvement
 
•
 
 حرية الفكر
والتعبير 
 
         
Freedom of thought and Expression
 
•
 
 احترام األفراد   
 
 
                       
Respect for the Individuals

 و يقوم كل قسم من األقسام

 و كذلك ترشيح القائم بالتدريس لكل مقرر دراسى سواء من اعضاء هيئة التدريس بالقسم ، من اعضاء
 هيئة التدريس بقسم اخر ، او من خارج الكلية . حيث  يكون تكليف القائمين بالتدريس من خارج الكلية
بعد موافقة لجنة شئون التعليم و الطالب بالكلية.

ا لباب الثاني 
 الهيكل التنظيمي 
( مادة7
): 
 هيكلة المقررات الدراسية 
 تهدف هيكلة المقررات الدراسية بكلية الهندسة إلى تنظيم المنهج الدراسي وتوفير تجربة تعليمية متكاملة
 للطالب. تم تقسيم المقررات إلى مستويات مختلفة، وتتضمن هذه المستويات متطلبات مختلفة تستند إلى اإلطار
 المرجعي
المصري بما يحقق رﺅية ورسالة واهداف الكلية وبرامجها المختلفة وتشمل : 
المستوى (أ): مقررات دراسية تحقق 
.متطلبات الجامعة 
 .المستوى (ب): مقررات دراسية تحقق متطلبات الكلية 
.المستوى (ج): مقررات دراسية تحقق متطلبات البرامج  
.المستوى (د): مقررات دراسية تحقق متطلبات التخصص الدقيق 
 تجدر االشارة الى أهمية أن يكون للطالب فهم جيد لهيكلة المقررات والمتطلبات الدراسية المطلوبة في كلية
 الهندسة، حيث يساعدهم ذلك في التخطيط لمسارهم األكاديمي والتأكد من استيفاء جميع المتطلبات الالزمة
 .للتخرج بنجاح 
( مادة8
)
 :
 متطلبات الجامعة 
ت شترك كلية الهندسة مع
باقي

والتكنولوجيا في 
 عدد (

(

)

 تمثل متطلبات الجامعة
والتي 
 تهدف الى بناء
 شخصية الخريج الثقافية، وتنمية مهارات الشخصية، واالدراك العام بقضايا،المجتمع 
 والتركيز على الهوية
واالرتباط بالوطن.

 مادة(
17
)
 : المرشد
األكادﻳمي  
بنا ءا على ترشيح مجلس البرنامج،المختص 
 تحدد الكلية لكل مجموعة من الطالب مرشدا أكاديميا من بين
 أعضاء هيئة التدريس بالكلية ويمكن االستعانة بالهيئة المعاونة كمرشدين أكاديميين بعد موافقة مجلس الكلية.  
 وتتلخص 
 مهام المرشد
األكاديمي في 
 النقاط :التالية 
•
 توجيه 
 الطالب
في 
 مسار
دراستهم ومساعدتهم 
 على اختيار المقررات الدراسية لكل فصل
دراسي .
 
•
 
 يقوم المرشد
األكاديمي 
 بتحديد عدد الساعات المعتمدة
التي 
 يمكنهم
تسجيلها وفقا 
 للمستوى األكاديمي 
 للطالب . 
•
 
 يقوم المرشد
األكاديمي بمساعدة الطالب على 
 حل المشكالت
التي 
 يواجهونها اثناء الدراسة
ومراقبة 
 تقدمهم
وادائهم 
 اثناء الفصل.الدراسي  
 
•
 
 يجوز للمرشد
األكاديمي 
 ان يطلب من الطالب إعادة أحد 
 او بعض المقررات
التي

Code: UNC01 
Subject: English Language 
Pre. Req.:  NA 
Credit Hours = 2 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture Exercises Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
0 
0 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus:  
Practice in specific points of grammar to consolidate and extend learner's existing knowledge - 
Analysis of syntax - comprehension - Skimming and scanning exercises develop the learners' skills - 
Comprehension questions interpretation and implication - the activities and games used develop 
listening - Speaking and writing skills through a communicative - Functional approach with suggested 
topics for discussion and exercises in summary writing and composition. 
References:  Nitin, B. (2010). Communicative English for Engineers and Professionals. Pearson 
Education India. 
 
Code: UNC02 
Subject: Social Issues 
Pre. Req.:  NA 
Credit Hours = 2 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture Exercises Practical 
Lecture 
Exercises 
2 
0 
0 
2 
0 
Course Syllabus:  
This course aims to introduce the student to the most important social and societal issues. The student 
discusses the causes of these problems, develops solutions for them, new visions for problems and 
issues, and develops the student's ability to face problems and issues. This course includes problems 
resulting from population increase, human rights, transparency and combating corruption, various 
topics include the impact of poverty, crime, drug addiction and prejudice on the individual and society. 
References: Thompson, J. (2008). A history of Egypt: from earliest times to the present. American 
Univ in Cairo Press. 
 
Code: UNC03 
Subject: Technical Writing 
Pre. Req.:  UNC01 
Credit Hours = 2 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture Exercises Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
0 
0 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus:  
This technical writing course covers types of reports (summary vs. detailed), report components, and 
their importance. It includes text and graphic writing techniques, report classification (technical and 
administrative), essential elements (abstract, summary, objectives, details, conclusions, 
recommendations, references), and logical report composition. Students will learn about grammar, 
punctuation, and using software like word processors and multimedia tools for effective 
communication. 
References: David F. Beer and David A. McMurrey (2019). A Guide to Writing as an Engineer, 5th 
edition, John Wiley & Sons, Inc.

Code: UNC04 
Subject: Communication and Presentation Skills 
Pre. Req.:  NA 
Credit Hours = 2 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture Exercises Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
0 
0 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus:  
This course develops communication and presentation skills for personal and professional success. 
Students learn verbal and written communication, including professional documents, presentations, 
and reports. The course covers technical writing, business letters, resumes, interpersonal 
communication, cultural sensitivities, active listening, persuasive communication, and interpreting 
cues. It equips students to handle various communication scenarios effectively, fostering stronger 
connections and achieving goals. 
References: “The College Writer: A Guide to Thinking, Writing, and Researching”, 5th Edition. 
by Randall VanderMey, Verne Meyer, John Van Rys , Patrick Sebranek

Subject: Critical Thinking

Lecture Exercises Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework

Course Syllabus:  
This course teaches logical and critical thinking, exploring the links between science, society, and 
daily life. By learning the scientific method and the role of scientific communities, students develop 
effective persuasive skills. 
References: Peter, F., & Gittens, C. A. (2015). Think Critically. 
 
Code: UNE06 
Ethics and Professionalism 
Pre. Req.:  NA 
Credit Hours = 2 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture Exercises Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
0 
0 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus:  
This course delves into the complexities of ethical matters in the professional and business world. 
Through its exploration of themes like integrity, civic duty, and ethical behavior, it examines both 
ethical lapses and responsible conduct. Additionally, it explores the rights and obligations of both 
employees and corporations, delving into the broader questions of social and economic justice within 
the global economic landscape. 
References: 
[1] A Concise Introduction to Ethics Concise Edition”, 2019 by Russ Shafer-Landau 
[2] Moral Choices: An Introduction to Ethics “, 2018, by Scott Rae

إ( عتمادا على المعايير األكاديمية القومية المرجعيةNARS 2018
) 
 فإن الخريج يجب أن يكون قادرا على
( اكتساب الجدارات األتية والخاصة بالعلوم الهندسيةLevel A
): 
A1. Identify, formulate, and solve complex engineering problems by applying 
engineering fundamentals, basic science, and mathematics. 
A2. Develop and conduct appropriate experimentation and/or simulation, 
analyze, and  interpret data, assess, and evaluate findings, and use statistical 
analyses and objective engineering judgment to draw conclusions. 
A3. Apply engineering design processes to produce cost-effective solutions that 
meet specified needs with consideration for global, cultural, social, economic, 
environmental, ethical, and  other aspects as appropriate to the discipline and 
within the principles and contexts of sustainable design and development. 
A4. Utilize contemporary technologies, codes of practice, and standards, quality 
guidelines, health and safety requirements, environmental issues, and risk 
management principles. 
A5. Practice research techniques and methods of investigation as an inherent part 
of learning. 
A6. Plan, supervise, and monitor implementation of engineering projects, taking 
into consideration other trades requirements. 
A7. Function efficiently as an individual and as a member of multi-disciplinary 
and multicultural teams. 
A8. Communicate effectively – graphically, verbally and in writing – with a range 
of audiences using contemporary tools. 
A9. Use creative, innovative, and flexible thinking and acquire entrepreneurial 
and leadership skills to anticipate and respond to new situations.

3. توصيف مقررات متطلبات الكلية 
Code:  BSC 111 
Subject: Engineering Mathematics 1 
Pre. Req.: NA 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
The course covers functions (definition, domain, range, types, even/odd, composite, inverse), calculus 
(limits, continuity, differentiation, integration, multivariable), and their applications in engineering. It also 
includes sequences and series (arithmetic, geometric, convergence, Taylor, Maclaurin), and analytic 
geometry (parametric equations, vector geometry, curvature, torsion, conic sections in polar coordinates). 
References: 
[1] Croft, A. (2017). Engineering Mathematics A Foundation for Electronic, Electrical, Communications 
and Systems Engineers. Pearson Education Limited. 
[2] Stroud, K. A., & Booth, D. J. (2020). Engineering mathematics. Bloomsbury Publishing. 
 
Code:  BSC 112 
Subject: Engineering Mathematics 2 
Pre. Req.: BSC 111 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
The course covers integration (definite, indefinite, techniques, improper, applications), multiple integrals 
(double integrals, polar coordinates, engineering applications), vector algebra (vector spaces, linear 
independence, inner product spaces, cross product, engineering applications), and matrix algebra (matrix 
operations, inverse matrices, eigenvalues, eigenvectors, engineering applications). 
References: 
[1] Croft, A. (2017). Engineering Mathematics A Foundation for Electronic, Electrical, Communications 
and Systems Engineers. Pearson Education Limited. 
[2] Stroud, K. A., & Booth, D. J. (2020). Engineering mathematics. Bloomsbury Publishing.

Code: ELE 171 
Subject: Introduction to Computer Programming 
Pre. Req.:  NA 
Credit Hours = 2 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture Exercises Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
1 
0 
2 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus:  
Basic programming concepts using one of the modern general-purpose programming languages. Basic 
input/output, Data types, Expressions, Mathematical and logical operators, Mathematical functions, 
Arrays, Multi-dimensional arrays, Strings, Enumerations, Conditions, Decisions, Loops, Functions, 
Function-call mechanisms, Recursive functions, Parameter passing, Addresses, Pointers/References, 
Pointers to pointers, Pointers to functions, Program memory segments, Dynamic allocations, Files, 
Error handling, Static and dynamic libraries. 
References: 
[1] D. S. Malik, C++ Programming: From Problem Analysis to Program Design, 8th Edition,  Course 
Technology, 2017.  
[2] Paul Deitel and Harvey Deitel, C++ How to Program, 7th Edition, 2010 by Pearson Education, 
Inc. 
 
Code:  BSC 121 
Subject: Physics 1 
Pre. Req.: NA 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework

Course Syllabus: 
Mechanics Fundamentals: Work, Energy, and Power (i.e., Fundamental principles, conservation laws, 
and practical applications)- Forces and Motion (i.e., Newtonian mechanics, force analysis, and vector 
dynamics)- Potential Gradient (Analysis of potential fields, gradient concepts, and implications).  
Vibrations and Oscillations: Circular Motion- Simple Harmonic Motion (SHM) - Harmonic 
Superposition- Damped Vibrations -Forced Oscillations and Resonance.  Wave Motion and Optics: 
Wave Equation -Wave Types- Sound Waves- Standing Waves-Light Interference and Diffraction. 
References: 
[1] Serway and Jewett, Physics for scientists and engineers with modern physics, 2014. 
[2] Halliday, D., Resnick, R., & Walker, J. (2013). Fundamentals of physics. John Wiley & Sons.

Course Syllabus:  
This course introduces fundamental project management concepts, offering an overview of 
organizational strategy, structure, and culture essential for effective project execution. Topics include 
project planning, scheduling, resource allocation, risk management, team building, cost estimation, 
project quality management, and supply chain integration. 
References: 
Siegel, N. G. (2020). Engineering Project Management. John Wiley & Sons.

Course Syllabus: 
An overview of project management, Terminology commonly used in project management, The 
various phases of a project and its life cycle, Managing project timelines, Utilizing tools like Gantt 
charts, Activity on Arrow, and Activity on Node, Understanding the relationships between project 
activities, Estimating project timelines using probability, Strategies for reducing project time through 
time crashing, Efficiently managing project resources, Implementing quality control measures to 
ensure project success. 
References: 
[1] Gray, C. F., & Larson, E. W. (2011). Project management the managerial process. McGraw-
Hill. 
[2] Rojas, E. M. (Ed.). (2009). Construction project management: A practical guide for building 
and electrical contractors. J. Ross Publishing.

Course Syllabus: 
The course covers project management basics, stakeholder engagement, project life cycle stages, 
feasibility studies, project selection methods, iterative planning and control, scope management, work 
breakdown structures, network diagrams, critical path method, bar charts, program evaluation and

Code: ENG 114 
Subject: Engineering Economy and Investments 
Pre. Req.:  NA 
Credit Hours = 2 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture Exercises Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
0 
0 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus: 
Introduction to engineering economy covers principles like the time value of money and interest rates. 
The course includes analyzing cash flows with present and future values, using cash flow diagrams. 
The course evaluates project options and assesses their worth using methods like payback period and 
internal rate of return. It also explores financial tools such as bond considerations, break-even points, 
and asset depreciation. Real-world applications illustrate economic analysis in engineering fields. 
References: 
Sullivan, W. G., Wicks, E. M., & Koelling, C. P. (2015). Engineering economy. Pearson. 
 
 
 
Code: ENG 115 
Subject: Structure and Properties of Materials 
Pre. Req.:  NA 
Credit Hours = 2 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture Exercises Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
1 
0 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus: 
Categorize engineering materials: metals, polymers, ceramics, and composites. Examine internal 
structures: atomic, micro, and macrostructure. Explore material properties: physical, chemical, 
mechanical, electrical, thermal, and optical. Understand the relationship between structure and 
properties. Investigate solidification, phase diagrams, and the iron-carbon diagram. Consider material-
environment interactions and study corrosion effects on materials. 
References: 
Trolier-McKinstry, S., & Newnham, R. E. (2018). Materials engineering: bonding, structure, and 
structure-property relationships. Cambridge University Press.

1. 
 رسالة البرنامج 
 يسعى برنامج هندسة األتمتة والروبوتات الى اعداد خريجين متميزين من خالل تخصصات بينية في هندسة
األتمتة والروبوتات، ليكونوا قادرين على المنافسة فى سوق العمل 
 محليا وإقليميا في التطبيقات العملية، ويكونوا
 .نموذجا يحتذى به في تطوير المجتمع وتنمية موارده 
2
 .
 أهداف
البرنامج 
 :يهدف برنامج هندسة األتمتة والروبوتات الى تقديم خريج قادر على 
1. 
  تطبيق المعرفة بالرياضيات الهندسية والعلوم الطبيعية واساسيات الهندسة و علوم الحاسب وقدرات
.التفكير التحليلي والنقدي والمنهجي لتحديد وحل المشكالت الهندسية 
2. 
 إكساب الطالب القدرة على التعامل مع أنظمة التحكم واألتمتة والروبوتات ألجل التعامل مع مشكلة
.تطبيقية محددة او لمتابعة التطوير فيها 
3. ت  شجيع العمل الجماعي بين  
 التخصصات والرﺅى المختلفة والمساهمة في تطوير المهنة والمجتمع
و
كساب الخريجين الصفات القيادية و مهارات ادارة المشروعات وريادة االعمال. . 
4. إ.عداد كوادر فنية للعمل في شتى مجاالت هندسة األتمتة الصناعية والروبوتات 
5. 
 تمكين الطالب من التعلم الذاتي المستمر الكتساب القدرة على إجراء البحث العلمي وتطوير القطاع
 .الصناعي وخدمة المجتمع 
3
  .مواصفات الخرﻳج 
 يحرص برنامج هندسة األتمتة والروبوتات على تخريج مهندسين متميزين ومؤهلين لدخول سوق العمل
 :ويراعى فيهم تحقيق المواصفات األتية 
1. Master a wide spectrum of engineering knowledge and specialized skills and can 
apply acquired knowledge using theories and abstract thinking in real life 
situations. 
2. Apply analytic critical and systemic thinking to identify, diagnose and solve 
engineering problems with a wide range of complexity and variation.  
3. Behave professionally and adhere to engineering ethics and standards.

4. Work in and lead a heterogeneous team of professionals from different 
engineering specialties and assume responsibility for own and team 
performance. 
5. Recognize his/her role in promoting the engineering field and contribute in the 
development of the profession and the community. 
6. Value the importance of the environment, both physical and natural, and work 
to promote sustainability principles. 
7. Use techniques, skills and modern engineering tools necessary for engineering 
practice. 
8. Assume full responsibility for own learning and self-development, engage in 
lifelong learning and demonstrate the capacity to engage in post- graduate and 
research studies. 
9. Communicate effectively using different modes, tools and languages with 
various audiences; to deal with academic/professional challenges in a critical 
and creative manner. 
10. Demonstrate leadership qualities, business administration and entrepreneurial 
skills.  
11. Design of automation and robotics systems considering the principles of 
mechanical, manufacturing, electrical/electronic, and computer engineering. 
12. Analyze and investigate the inter-disciplinary characteristics of mechanical, 
electrical, control and robotics systems. 
13. Work within a multidisciplinary team during the analysis, design and 
implementation phases of automation and robotics engineering projects.

Code:  BSC 215 
Subject: Differential Equations 
Pre. Req.: BSC 112 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
Definition and classification of differential equations- First-Order Ordinary Differential Equations- 
Second-Order Ordinary Differential Equations- Higher-Order Ordinary Differential Equations- Partial 
Differential Equations- Laplace transform and its applications -Numerical Methods for Differential 
Equations-Engineering applications 
References: 
S. A. Wirkus, and R. J. Swifi, "A Course of Ordinary Differential Equations", Taylor & Francis Group, 
LLC, 2015. 
 
Code:  BSC 216 
Subject: Numerical Analysis 
Pre. Req.: BSC 112 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
Numerical Analysis:  Sources of error in numerical computation, Round-off error and truncation error, 
Accuracy, and convergence of numerical algorithms.   Numerical Differentiation and Integration: Finite 
difference approximations, Richardson extrapolation, Newton-Cotes formulas: Trapezoidal rule, Simpson's 
rule, Gaussian quadrature. Difference equations:   Definition and classification of difference equations, 
First-order and higher-order difference equations, Solutions of difference equations: homogeneous and 
nonhomogeneous cases. Z-Transform.  Fourier analysis: Fourier series representation of periodic 
functions, Fourier transform and inverse Fourier transform. Engineering applications. 
References: 
[1] Burden, R. L. (2011). Numerical analysis. Brooks/Cole Cengage Learning. 
[2] Hamming, R. W. (2012). Introduction to applied numerical analysis. Courier Corporation.

Course Syllabus: 
Elements of electrical circuits - Simple resistive circuits - Analysis of DC circuits - Theories of electrical 
circuits – Capacitors and Inductors - First-Order Circuits - AC fundamentals -AC Circuits Sinusoidal Steady 
- State Analysis - Three-Phase Circuits-   Power and power factor- Resonance circuits - Three-phase circuits.

Code:  ELE 374

Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2

Course Syllabus: 
Basics of digital signal processing-  Characterization, and classification of Discrete-Time (DT) signals and 
systems- Typical DT signal processing operations - Introduction to digital filters-  Digital Filter Structures  
–Finite-impulse response (FIR) digital filter design - Infinite-impulse response (IIR) digital filter design –

Subject: Rehabilitation Robots

Course Syllabus: 
Introduction to rehabilitation robots - Assistive robotics terminology – Assistive robotic control systems 
including mechanical design - Sensors & actuators, and user interfaces - Rehabilitative robotics for 
neurologic injuries such as stroke - Prosthetics to enable mobility function in upper and lower limb 
amputees - Social robotics for cognitive impairments and developmental disorders (such as autism) - 
Guidelines for designing assistive robots - Ethical and regulatory considerations in the design of 
assistive robots. 
References 
[1] Pedro Encarnação and Albert Cook (Editors), “Robotic Assistive Technologies: Principles and Practice 
(Rehabilitation Science in Practice Series)” 1st Edition, 2017. 
 
Code: ELE 477 
Subject: Fundamentals of Biomedical Engineering Pre. Req.:  NA 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
1 
1 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus: 
The Biomedical Engineering course covers tissue engineering, artificial organs, and prosthetics, 
including lower and upper limb designs. It includes basics of medical instrumentation, biological 
signals, and biomedical sensors (e.g., ECG, EEG). Topics include bio signal processing, physiological 
origins, signal acquisition, frequency domain representation, Fourier and wavelet analysis, sampling, 
filtering, and EKG analysis. The course also addresses medical imaging techniques. 
References 
Joseph J. Carr, John M. Brown, "Introduction to Biomedical Equipment Technology” 4th Edition - ISBN-
10: 0130104922.

Code: ELE 416 
Subject: Fault Detection and Diagnosis in 
Industrial Processes 
Pre. Req.:  NA 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus: 
This course provides engineering undergraduates with a thorough understanding of fault detection and 
diagnosis in industrial processes. It covers fundamental principles, methodologies, and tools for detecting 
and diagnosing faults in both static and dynamic processes. Key topics include basic concepts, model-
based, data-driven, and machine learning techniques for fault detection and diagnosis. Through lectures, 
practical exercises, and case studies, students will gain skills to effectively identify and analyze faults in 
various industrial applications. 
References 
[1] Ding, Steven X. Advanced methods for fault diagnosis and fault-tolerant control. Springer Berlin 
Heidelberg, 2021. 
[2] Isermann, Rolf. Fault-diagnosis systems: an introduction from fault detection to fault tolerance. Springer 
Science & Business Media, 2006. 
[3] Chiang, Leo H., Evan L. Russell, and Richard D. Braatz. Fault detection and diagnosis in industrial 
systems. Springer Science & Business Media, 2000.

 رسالة البرنامج 
يسعي 
 برنامج هندسة ميكاترونيات السيارات 
 إلعداد مهندسين متميزين ومؤهلين من الناحيتين العلمية 
 والعملية
والمهنية في مجال 
 ميكاترونيات السيارات لتلبية 
 احتياجات سوق 
،العمل قادرين على نقل 
،المعارف 
 وتوطين 
،التقنية

 البحث

 االستشارات  الهندسية  المتخصصة  لتطوير  
 المجتمع 
الصناعي وخدمة المجتمع المحلي واإلقليمي. 
2. 
 أهداف البرنامج 
 يهدف برنامج هندسة ميكاترونيات السيارات 
 :الى تقديم خريج قادر على 
1. 
  تطبيق المعرفة بالرياضيات الهندسية والعلوم الطبيعية واساسيات الهندسة و علوم الحاسب وقدرات
.التفكير التحليلي والنقدي والمنهجي لتحديد وحل المشكالت الهندسية 
2. 
 .تنمية القدرات البشرية لسد حاجة المجتمع الصناعي من مهندسي ميكاترونيات السيارات 
3. 
 المشاركة في تحقيق .الخطة الوطنية للتنمية واستخدام المعرفة لخدمتها بهدف تنمية المجتمع 
4. 
 االسهام في رفع الكفاءة المهنية وتكوين جيل من المهندسين المتميزين والباحثين المؤهلين في مجال
.هندسة ميكاترونيات السيارات 
5. 
 تمكين الطالب من التعلم الذاتي المستمر الكتساب القدرة على إجراء البحث العلمي وتطوير القطاع
 .الصناعي وخدمة المجتمع 
3. مواصفات الخرﻳج 
 يحرص برنامج هندسة ميكاترونيات السيارات 
 على تخريج مهندسين متميزين ومؤهلين لدخول سوق العمل
 :ويراعى فيهم تحقيق المواصفات األتية 
1. Master a wide spectrum of engineering knowledge and specialized skills and can 
apply acquired knowledge using theories and abstract thinking in real life 
situations. 
2. Apply analytic critical and systemic thinking to identify, diagnose and solve 
engineering problems with a wide range of complexity and variation.  
3. Behave professionally and adhere to engineering ethics and standards.

4. 
 المعاﻳير األكادﻳمية للبرنامج 
( باإلضافة الى الجدارات لمعظم البرامج الهندسيةLevel A
 ) وبالتوافق مع المعايير األكاديمية المرجعية
(
NARS 2018) فأن 
 خريجي برنامج هندسة ميكاترونيات السيارات يتعين أن يكونوا قادرين على تحقيق
(
Level B - Competencies for Mechanical Engineering
 :) على النحو التالي 
B1. Model, analyze and design physical systems applicable to the specific discipline 
by applying the concepts of: Thermodynamics, Heat Transfer, Fluid Mechanics, 
solid Mechanics, Material Processing, Material Properties, Measurements, 
Instrumentation, Control Theory and Systems, Mechanical Design and Analysis, 
Dynamics and Vibrations.  
B2. Plan, manage and carry out designs of mechanical systems and machine 
elements using appropriate materials both traditional means and computer-aided 
tools and software contemporary to the mechanical engineering field.  
B3. Select conventional mechanical equipment according to the required 
performance.  
B4. Adopt suitable national and international standards and codes; and integrate 
legal, economic and financial aspects to: design, build, operate, inspect and 
maintain mechanical equipment and systems. 
 
( باإلضافة الىLevel A and Level B) فأن 
 خريجي برنامج هندسة ميكاترونيات السيارات يتعين أن يكونوا
( قادرين على تحقيقLevel C - Competencies for Automative Mechatronic Engineering
 )
 :على النحو التالي 
C1. Illustrate the basic concepts and theories of mathematics, sciences, engineering 
projection and their applications within the field of Automative mechatronics 
engineering. 
C2. Analyze automative mechatronic systems using scientific, mathematical and 
computer-based models and asses the limitations of particular cases.

C3. Identify and discuss vehicles as complex systems from technical and social

Subject: Differential Equations

Credit Hours = 3 hrs.

Exercises

Course Syllabus: 
Definition and classification of differential equations- First-Order Ordinary Differential Equations- Second-
Order Ordinary Differential Equations- Higher-Order Ordinary Differential Equations- Partial Differential 
Equations- Laplace transform and its applications -Numerical Methods for Differential Equations-
Engineering applications 
References:

Code:  BSC 216 
Subject: Numerical Analysis 
Pre. Req.: BSC 112 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
Numerical Analysis:  Sources of error in numerical computation, Round-off error and truncation error, 
Accuracy, and convergence of numerical algorithms.   Numerical Differentiation and Integration: Finite 
difference approximations, Richardson extrapolation, Newton-Cotes formulas: Trapezoidal rule, Simpson's 
rule, Gaussian quadrature. Difference equations:   Definition and classification of difference equations, 
First-order and higher-order difference equations, Solutions of difference equations: homogeneous and 
nonhomogeneous cases. Z-Transform.  Fourier analysis: Fourier series representation of periodic 
functions, Fourier transform and inverse Fourier transform. Engineering applications. 
References: 
[1] Burden, R. L. (2011). Numerical analysis. Brooks/Cole Cengage Learning. 
[2] Hamming, R. W. (2012). Introduction to applied numerical analysis. Courier Corporation.

Code:  ELE 273 
Subject: Logic Design 
Pre. Req.: NA 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
0 
2 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
Introduction to logic circuits - Logic circuit simplification using Boolean algebra, DeMorgan theorem, and 
K-Map. Combinational logic circuits like adders, subtractors, decoders, encoders, multiplexers, and de-
multiplexers. Sequential logic circuits like Flip-Flops, counters (synchronous and asynchronous), and 
registers. The course also explores design methodologies, programmable logic devices, and gate arrays. 
Hands-on practice with state tables, state transition diagrams, and counter design is included. 
 
References: 
M Morris, M., & Michael, D. (2013). Digital Design With an Introduction to the Verilog HDL FIFTH 
EDITION

Code: MEE 214 
Subject: Strength of Materials 
Pre. Req.:  NA 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture Exercises Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
1 
1 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus: 
This course covers loads on mechanical parts, equilibrium analysis, and types of forces like axial, shear, 
bending, and torsion. It includes stress, strain, and Hooke's Law, as well as design stress, safety factors, and 
stress concentration. Topics include thermal stresses, bearing stresses, and shear stresses in beams, along 
with beam deflection, two-dimensional stress analysis using Mohr’s Circle, power transmission shafts, 
eccentric loads, column buckling, and thin-walled vessels. 
References 
[1] Beer, Ferdinand, John DeWolf, E. Russell Johnston Jr, and David Mazurek. ("Mechanics of materials." 
2014. 
 
Code: MEE 216 
Subject: Computer Aided Design 
Pre. Req.:  MEE 111 
Credit Hours = 2 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
1 
0 
2 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus: 
The computer-Aided Design (CAD) course covers CAD software fundamentals, including interface 
navigation, 2D drawing, and geometric construction. It progresses to 3D modeling, assembly modeling, 
and rendering techniques. Topics include orthographic projections, dimensioning, advanced modeling 
techniques, and CAD file management. Students will engage in hands-on projects, exploring real-world 
engineering applications. The course also introduces CAM integration and future CAD trends, providing 
a comprehensive understanding of CAD in engineering. 
References 
[1] Randy H. Shih, "Learning SOLIDWORKS 2023: Modeling, Assembly and Analysis", SDC 
Publications, 2023. 
[2] David Planchard, "Engineering Design with SOLIDWORKS", SDC Publications, 2021.

Course Syllabus:

Course Syllabus:

automotive engines, along with the construction and operation of fuel delivery systems. Discusses fuel

Catalysis - Prospects of the DMFC – Applications - Stationary Power Generation - Portable Systems - 
Fuel Cell Vehicles - Competing Technologies for Transportation - Energy Efficiency Of Fuel Cell Systems 
- The Current Market Situation. 
References 
Gregor Hoogers, Fuel Cell Technology Handbook, CRC Press, 2003.

  المتخصصة المطلوبة في مجال هندسة الطاقة الجديدة،والمستدامة  و  قادرين على  المنافسة فى سوق العمل

 أهداف البرنامج

 المشاركة بنشاط
في

وتنمية

.التفكير التحليلي والنقدي والمنهجي لتحديد وحل المشكالت الهندسية 
2. 
 تزويد الخريجين بالمهارات الالزمة للتعامل مع أنظمة الطاقة الكهربائية، مع التركيز على أنظمة
.الطاقة المتجددة والمستدامة

ة

 غرس القيم المهنية واألخالقية في نفوس الخريجين وتمكينهم من أن يصبحوا قادة في مختلف
.قطاعات صناعة الطاقة

 تعزيز ثقافة التعلم مدى الحياة، وتمكين الخريجين من تطوير معارفهم ومهاراتهم بشكل مستمر
.من خالل التعلم الذاتي المستمر 
6. 
 إعداد الخريجين للدراسات العليا والبحث العلمي من خالل تعزيز التفكير اإلبداعي، وتحليل
 .المشكالت، ومهارات حل المشكالت بشكل منهجي

1. Master a wide spectrum of engineering knowledge and specialized skills and 
can apply acquired knowledge using theories and abstract thinking in real life 
situations. 
2. Apply analytic critical and systemic thinking to identify, diagnose and solve 
engineering problems with a wide range of complexity and variation. 
3. Behave professionally and adhere to engineering ethics and standards. 
4. Work in and lead a heterogeneous team of professionals from different 
engineering specialties and assume responsibility for own and team 
performance. 
5. Recognize his/her role in promoting the engineering field and contribute to 
the development of the profession and the community. 
6. Value the importance of the environment, both physical and natural, and work 
to promote sustainability principles. 
7. Use techniques, skills, and modern engineering tools necessary for 
engineering practice. 
8. Assume full responsibility for own learning and self-development, engage in 
lifelong learning and demonstrate the capacity to engage in post- graduate and 
research studies. 
9. Communicate effectively using different modes, tools, and languages with 
various audiences; to deal with academic/professional challenges in a critical 
and creative manner. 
10. Demonstrate leadership qualities, business administration and entrepreneurial 
skills. 
11. Integrate knowledge of renewable energy sciences with various engineering 
disciplines.  
12. Develop models and designs for integrated energy systems that incorporate 
multiple disciplines.

13. Design, implement, operate, and maintain renewable energy facilities, while 
also conducting specialized research and studies within the energy sector.  
4. 
 المعاﻳير األكادﻳمية للبرنامج 
باإلضافة الى الجدارات لمعظم البرامج الهندسية (
Level A
) 
 فأن برنامج هندسة الطاقة
المتجددة والمستدامة 
 له بعض الجدارات الخاصة وهي كما يلي : 
D1. Evaluate and apply principles of thermodynamics, heat transfer, fluid 
mechanics, control theory, and mechanical design to select, model, and analyze 
renewable energy systems tailored to specific disciplines. 
D2. Design, model, and analyze electrical, electronic, mechanical, or digital 
components for renewable energy applications, utilizing appropriate 
optimization tools. 
D3. Assess and measure the performance of electrical, electronic, mechanical, or 
digital systems and circuits under defined input conditions to determine their 
suitability for renewable energy utilization. 
D4. Analyze, model, and select electrical power systems relevant to renewable and 
sustainable energy applications, integrating principles of power generation, 
transmission, and distribution. 
D5. Incorporate relevant national and international standards and codes into the 
design, construction, operation, inspection, and maintenance of electrical, 
electronic, mechanical, or digital equipment and systems for renewable energy 
applications. 
5. 
 المقررات الدراسية 
 للحصول على درجة

Code:  BSC 215 
Subject: Differential Equations 
Pre. Req.: BSC 112 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
Definition and classification of differential equations- First-Order Ordinary Differential Equations- Second-
Order Ordinary Differential Equations- Higher-Order Ordinary Differential Equations- Partial Differential 
Equations- Laplace transform and its applications -Numerical Methods for Differential Equations-
Engineering applications 
References: 
S. A. Wirkus, and R. J. Swifi, "A Course of Ordinary Differential Equations", Taylor & Francis Group, 
LLC, 2015. 
 
Code:  BSC 216 
Subject: Numerical Analysis 
Pre. Req.: BSC 112 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
Numerical Analysis:  Sources of error in numerical computation, Round-off error and truncation error, 
Accuracy, and convergence of numerical algorithms.   Numerical Differentiation and Integration: Finite 
difference approximations, Richardson extrapolation, Newton-Cotes formulas: Trapezoidal rule, Simpson's 
rule, Gaussian quadrature. Difference equations:   Definition and classification of difference equations, 
First-order and higher-order difference equations, Solutions of difference equations: homogeneous and 
nonhomogeneous cases. Z-Transform.  Fourier analysis: Fourier series representation of periodic 
functions, Fourier transform and inverse Fourier transform. Engineering applications. 
References: 
[1] Burden, R. L. Numerical analysis. Brooks/Cole Cengage Learning, 2011 
[2] Hamming, R. W. Introduction to applied numerical analysis. Courier Corporation, 2012.

Code:  ELE 211 
Subject: Electrical Circuits 
Pre. Req.: BSC 122 
Credit Hours = 4 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
3

50% 
Course Syllabus: 
Elements of electrical circuits - Simple resistive circuits - Analysis of DC circuits - Theories of electrical 
circuits – Capacitors and Inductors - First-Order Circuits - AC fundamentals -AC Circuits Sinusoidal Steady 
- State Analysis - Three-Phase Circuits-   Power and power factor- Resonance circuits - Three-phase circuits. 
References: 
[1] Charles K. Alexander and Matthew N. O. Sadiku, Fundamentals of Electric Circuits, McGraw-Hill, 
Fourth Edition, 2009. 
[2] J. W. Nilsson, "Electric Circuits", Pearson; 11th edition, 2018. 
 
Code:  ELE 212 
Subject: Electronics 
Pre. Req.: BSC 122 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
1 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
Semiconductors: Overview of PN junctions, their biasing, and types of diodes.  Bipolar Junction Transistors 
(BJTs): properties, and applications in DC circuits. Field-Effect Transistors (JFET & MOSFET):  properties 
and applications. Introduction to CMOS technology and its applications. 
References: 
[1] Thomas L. Floyd David L. Buchla “Electronics Fundamentals Circuits, Devices and Applications” , 
Eighth Edition, PEARSON, 2014.  
[2] T. Floyd, "Electronic Devices", 10th edition, Pearson, 2018

Code: ELE 388 
Subject: Energy Efficiency in Buildings 
Pre. Req.:  ELE 251 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus: 
The course covers energy efficiency in buildings, focusing on lighting, heat and moisture transport, and 
hygrothermal properties of materials. It includes U-value and thermal capacity calculations, heat loss 
estimation, insulation practices, and thermal comfort. Topics also cover HVAC systems, renewable 
energy integration, ventilation, air quality, and airtightness. Principles of bioclimatic design and energy 
performance certification are also addressed to ensure comprehensive understanding and application of 
sustainable building practices. 
References 
Sweeney, James L. Energy efficiency: building a clean, Secure Economy. Hoover Press, 2016. 
 
 
Code: MEE 221 
Subject: Fluid Mechanics 
Pre. Req.:  BSC 215 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus: 
Fluid properties – Fluid statics and kinematics – Flow in pipes – Pumps – Valves – Dimensionless 
analysis and similitude– Buoyancy – Kinematics of three-dimensional fluid motions – Fluid masses 
moving with acceleration – Vortex motion – Hydrodynamics – Momentum equation - Euler’s and 
Bernoulli’s equations. 
References 
[1] B. R. Munson, D. F. Young and A. T. Okiishi, "Fundamentals of Fluid Mechanics ," 4th edition, John 
Wiley and Sons, Inc., New York, 2002. 
[2] F. M. White, "Fluid Mechanics," 7th Edition,  McGraw-Hill, 2011.

Code: MEE 223 
Subject: Thermodynamics 
Pre. Req.:  BSC 121 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50 % 
Course Syllabus: 
Heat and work, first law of thermodynamics, properties of steam and gases, steam tables and charts, 
entropy, second law, air standard cycle, compressors, psychrometry of gas mixtures, reversibility, 
availability and second law efficiency, gas turbine power cycles, steam turbine power cycles, combined 
gas-vapor power cycles. 
References 
[1] Yunus A. Cengel and Michael A. Boles, Thermodynamics: an engineering approach, 8th edition, New 
York: McGraw-Hill.  
[2] Borgnakke, Claus, and Richard E. Sonntag, Fundamentals of Thermodynamics, Wiley Global Education, 
9th edition. 
 
Code:  ELE 215 
Subject: Electromagnetic Fields 
Pre. Req.: BSC 121 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 %

Course Syllabus: 
Electrostatic Fields - Electric flux and gauss's law- Electrical Potential – Electric boundary conditions – 
Dipole moment – Capacitance - Poisson and Laplace equations – Piot & savart law- Amperes law- Vector 
potentials- Magnetic boundary conditions- Magnetic flux – Force and energy in magnetic field- Coils – Time 
varying field- Maxwell's equations- Wave propagation – Introduction to transmission line theory- Propagation 
in losses and lossless media- Reflection and refraction. Electromagnetics in AC electrical machines. 
References: 
[1] M. N. O. Sadiku, “Elements of Electromagnetics, “ 6th Ed, Oxford Press, 2006. 
[2] William H. Hayt, Johan A. Buck, “Engineering Electromagnetics” 8th Ed., McGraw Hill, 2012.

Code:  ELE 231 
Subject: Electrical Power Engineering 
Pre. Req.: ELE 215 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
Introduction to electric power systems - definition of active and reactive power - transmission lines 
components - characteristics and performance of transmission lines - Overhead lines - mechanical design of 
overhead transmission lines - underground cables - design of electrical distribution systems- generation of 
high voltage- measurement of high voltage - electric insulation types- corona- earthing and safety- 
Introduction to power system planning. 
References: 
[1] Sadhu, P.K. and S. Das, Elements of Power Systems. CRC Press, 2015. 
[2] Weedy, B.M., et al., Electric power systems. John Wiley & Sons, 2012.

Course Syllabus:

Code:  RSE 446 
Subject: Tidal and Wave Energy Engineering 
Pre. Req.: NA 
Credit Hours = 3 hrs. 
Assessments 
Lecture 
Exercises 
Practical 
Final Exam 
Coursework 
2 
2 
0 
50 % 
50% 
Course Syllabus: 
This course delves into the principles, technologies, and applications of tidal and wave energy as renewable 
resources for sustainable energy production. Students will explore the fundamentals of tidal energy, including 
tidal energy principles, analysis of tidal energy resources, and the interplay between tidal and wind energy. 
Additionally, the course covers tidal energy classification, with a focus on Egypt's tidal energy potential and 
utilization prospects. 
References: 
[1] Greaves, D., Iglesias, G., & Wiley, J. Wave and tidal energy. John Wiley & Sons Limited, 2018. 
[2] Khare, V., Khare, C., Nema, S., & Baredar, P. Tidal Energy Systems: design, optimization and control. 
Elsevier, 2018.
